\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    checkpoint_path = os.path.join(path, f\\\"{module_name}--{step}_checkpoint.pt\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Loading checkpoint: {checkpoint_path}\\\")\\n    state_dict = def load_checkpoint(module_name: str, path: str, step: int, device: str = "cpu") -> dict:
    checkpoint_path = os.path.join(path, f"{module_name}--{step}_checkpoint.pt")
    state_dict = torch.load(checkpoint_path, weights_only=True, map_location=device)
    return remove_ddp_in_checkpoint(state_dict)
```

And at line 271:


When resume is enabled, it loads checkpoints from the vla_path directory using the naming pattern `{module_name}--{step}_checkpoint.pt`. The s1 7500 checkpoint has the action_head, occ_head, and proprio_projector modules available, but the base model itself comes from cfg.vla_path.

The problem is that vla_path serves two conflicting purposes: it's where the base model loads from initially, and it's also where resume checkpoints are expected to be found. If I point vla_path to the incomplete 7500 checkpoint directory, the base model loading fails because config.json is missing. If I point it to the base model directory instead, the resume logic won't find the checkpoint files there. This creates a catch-22 where neither approach works, so I'll need to start fresh from the base model.
````
